Part 1

Nineteen-year-old Maya had arrived at her bus stop a little annoyed. A few days before this moment, she had kindly lent her car to her sixteen-year-old little brother, Mason, and he'd had an accident with it. Making it unusable without a trip to the garage.

She was therefore forced to take the bus to her fencing practice across town, every day after her university classes.

She nonchalantly boarded the bus with her enormous bag and settled into a corner, hoping not to disturb anyone. The last time she'd taken the bus had been what seemed like a millennium ago.

When the bus started moving again, she blew out a breath before scanning the interior with her eyes.

Infant - Arf, the horror.

However, her eyebrows had raised a little more in surprise, and her eyes had flickered to make sure her vision wasn't skewed.

When the gaze at the other end of the bus had met her gaze in return, she'd swallowed before quickly turning her head away.

This woman couldn't possibly be as beautiful as she'd glimpsed. It was impossible. Women with this beauty, such beauty, were usually actresses - with a ton of make-up - or mannequins, singers, models - but not a total stranger, on a bus after five-thirty, in Seattle.

Her eyes had risen again to this stranger, and this time it was the woman in front of her who had to look away.

Was she looking at me? Maybe, she thinks I'm carrying a weapon or - well, okay, technically it is a weapon - but...

Maya had swallowed again, detailing this stranger's face from a distance, watching her lips pushed slightly forward. The curve of her nose. Her perfect profile, right down to the waves of her hair.

She had bitten her lip and, just as her body was dying to cross the bus, this stranger's phone must have rung, because Maya had seen her smile and her lips immediately parted to start a conversation, her headphones pressed into her ears.

For a moment, Maya wondered if the vision of this celestial being had broken her, for she could hear her speaking, despite the fact that she was trying to whisper and the words coming out of her mouth sounded to her like an invented language.

She had shaken her head and, as if her neurons had reconnected with this simple gesture, she had recognized, she didn't know how to speak Spanish or Italian, but it sounded like something of the sort.

In fact, this accent was much nicer than Andy's, her best friend, and her father's, when they spoke. So it had to be Italian.

The young woman - probably Italian, then - had started laughing, offering the most beautiful laugh and smile Maya had ever heard or seen, and she'd had trouble swallowing her saliva, once again.

She'd spent the end of her bus ride looking at this stranger, or turning her head away to just listen, when his gaze landed on her in return.

However, she'd had to watch this stranger get off the bus, with no certainty that she'd ever see her again.

She'd let out one last sigh, wondering if she'd really seen the most beautiful woman in the world on that bus.

When she'd left for her best friend's house after practice - like many an evening - she'd plopped down on the sofa with a huff, resting her head back on the backrest.

- "Rough day?" asked Andy.

Maya had straightened her head.

- "No, I..." she'd stopped herself, searching for words.

"I think I'm in shock, actually."

- "In shock?"

- "I... I think I ran into with the most beautiful woman in the world."

- "Ran into?" asked Maya, frowning slightly.

- "You know I have to take the bus - because of Mase; and believe it or not, I never thought I'd say this; but I think it's the most beautiful thing in the world that's ever happened to me."

- "And what's this stranger's name?"

- "again, believe it or not, I have absolutely no idea."

Maya had once again dropped her head back onto the back of the sofa.

"She's so beautiful, I was literally nailed to the floor... well, the floor of the bus."

- "Maya Bishop, unable to approach a girl?" asked Andy, raising an eyebrow.

"You really expect me to believe that?! ... Is this for a hidden camera? To find out how well I know my best friend?!"

Maya had straightened up again, looking shocked:

- "I swear. She..."

Her eyes had gone blank, as if she could see this stranger before her eyes again.

"She's not human..."

- "And so she takes the bus?"

- "Yeah, she was already on the bus when I got on... And she got off about twenty minutes later, two stops from mine..."

She had paused.

"I swear, she's... physically she's perfect... And..."

She'd blushed.

"She has a beautiful voice."

- "Was she talking to someone?"

- "She got a phone call..."

She'd frowned before pushing her lips forward, as the stranger had done, as if thinking.

"But she didn't speak English..."

She'd raised her eyes to her best friend's, as if she had to be rational:

"It wasn't Spanish either. I think it was Italian."

- "Houuu I see mademoiselle Bishop has expensive tastes!"

- "Why do you say that?"

- "Italian women are kind of reputed to be the most beautiful - well, Italians men too, like the ones with the most class and poise!"

- "I can confirm that. If this girl is - sorry this woman, is indeed Italian - she should be elected the most beautiful woman in the world."

- "So finally, the bus is a good option?"

- "I'll tell you that if I run into her again!"

- "And you'll talk to her?"

- "If my feet are cooperative..."

- "And you think your mouth will be - or at least your brain - will be cooperative with your feet."

Maya had straightened up, opening her eyes wide, as if having a revelation.

- "Stop! the anguish! I can't go and talk to her! imagine I'm babbling, or I can't string two words together...! I've never swallowed before when looking at a girl!"

She leaned back on the sofa again.

"Woman - sorry it's a woman. She's clearly older than me. I mean, I don't want to say she's thirty or more. But she's definitely not «just twenty»."

Maya had huffed:

"With my luck, she's straight..."

- "Have you finished Calimero?!"

- "No, you're right! With my luck I'd never run into her again, so what's the point of imagining she's straight, or remembering that she grounded me?!"

Maya had dropped her head back and closed her eyes.

- "You don't really seem against the idea of her coming and literally grounded you down ..."

The blonde had lifted her head, opening one eye.

- "Ha, ha, ha very funny..."

- "Admit it, you wouldn't mind being tackled to the ground by that pretty Italian girl, even if it is against the hard floor of the bus..."

- "Or the other way around..."

- "Or the other way around!" confirmed Andy.

The next day, once again, Maya had made her way to her bus stop - the D, and once again she had boarded it once ahead of her. She had moved forward and after validating her ticket, her eyes had scanned the interior of the bus, but she hadn't been able to help feeling a little twinge of guilt at not seeing the stranger from the day before. She had moved forward with her fencing bag and her heart had only skipped a beat when she heard a melodious laugh and turned her head towards the person «hiding» behind a glass wall, carrying an advertising poster.

The stranger was there. Still on the phone, but this time, instead of sitting, she was standing, leaning.

Maya had positioned herself in the same spot as the day before, and from there she could observe her even more closely.

Her laughter, the movement of her lips, her accent, all magnificent, even if everything was happening - in her opinion - in slow motion.

The stranger must have felt Maya's gaze on her, for she raised her eyes to her. Blushing slightly.

- "Sorry," she said in the most sublime of accents, before lowering her voice, immediately returning to her conversation - in Italian.

The blonde had wanted to reply, but had been surprised. She had averted her eyes, embarrassed.

Shit, shit, shit, I've got to stop staring at her!

Wait... was she this beautiful yesterday?

Maya had looked at her stranger again, before lowering her eyes.

Stop looking at her like that!

The blonde had tried to keep her gaze away from this stranger, but she kept coming back to her, attracted by her beauty.

The brunette had been on the phone the whole way, and when she got out, her conversation was still ongoing.

For the rest of the week, every time Maya got on the bus, this stranger had been there. And even though she hadn't been on the phone every time, the blonde hadn't been able to bring herself to talk to her.

On Saturday, when she'd called the garage, she'd almost been glad when the mechanic told her they hadn't had a chance to look at her car yet. If they hadn't had time, then she was going to have to keep taking the bus.

On Monday, she had seen herself running to make sure she caught the bus with the beautiful stranger. Indeed, her teacher had wanted to keep her for a while after class to talk about her latest assignment. However, the only thing on her mind had been to catch that bus, the one that passed every day at the same time.

When she boarded the bus, after validating her ticket, she couldn't help but smile. The pretty stranger was sitting at the back of the bus. Her back was to her, but she recognized her immediately.

When the bus had started moving again, she had crossed it and gone to stick herself at the back, opposite this young woman, standing behind the last row of seats.

She leaned back and studied - once again - the features of her face. And once again her mouth. She seemed to be concentrating on reading something, and Maya reflected that she'd never seen anyone read so perfectly. This young woman's lips were slightly pushed forward, as if it helped her think a little more. And the blonde couldn't help biting her bottom lip.

The stranger on the bus must have sensed that someone was watching her, because she raised her face, meeting the blonde's gaze. The Italian-speaking woman smiled, and Maya felt her heart skip a beat in her chest. Her cheeks had tingled slightly and she hadn't been able to keep her lips from lifting at the corners.

Why did this stranger have to make her smile just by looking at her? and again, even just by seeing her back on the bus. It didn't make sense. She who was always self-assured, always charming; she felt destabilized by the mere presence of this person a few meters away from her.

Perhaps without realizing it. Perhaps on purpose. The young woman who spoke Italian had let her gaze fall on Maya's body. Or perhaps simply on her outfit. She had slightly moistened her lips - with her tongue - and the blonde's heart had started to race. Her mind wondered if this gesture was intended for her, and if so, what it meant.

Then the Italian had slowly moved her gaze back up to Maya's and, seeing her face slightly rosy, she had smiled a little more. She had lowered her gaze again to her Kindle to resume her reading. And as the blonde had opened her lips to finally manage to approach her, she had seen this stranger slip her hand into her pocket and pick up her phone with a huge smile.

- "Pronta tesoro? come stai?"

Maya's shoulders had slumped slightly and she'd let herself fall back against the wall of the bus, turning her face to the view of Seattle that flashed before her eyes. It wasn't going to happen again today.

When the young Italian got up to get off the bus, Maya couldn't help but watch her leave the space they shared, a sweet bitterness in her throat. However, once again her heart had gone into extrasystole, for just before leaving the bus, this stranger's hand had caressed the glass of the bus door and her face had turned towards Maya, sketching a smile in her direction. The young woman, who appeared to be Italian - with her perfectly mastered accent - had bitten her lip slightly, despite the interaction she was having on the phone, when she saw the blonde's face light up at her smile.

When the Italian had left her field of vision, the blonde had slumped back against the metal wall of the bus, before huffing slightly.

When, on Tuesday, Maya had boarded the bus, her fencing bag on her back - fortunately she didn't have to carry her huge bag, the one with wheels, that she usually had when her car wasn't being repaired - she'd seen her. She approached with a smile on her face. It was decided today, she was going to approach her.

However, her smile had quickly faded when she'd seen her almost black gaze, and seen the headphones in her ears.

- "No papà! Non se ne parla proprio di tornare in Italia per studiare medicina. Non ho nemmeno fatto gli studi adeguati prima..." [No dad! There's no way I'm going back to Italy to study medicine. I haven't even done the proper studies before].

There was a blank. Probably the other person on the phone was answering, but the further her arguments went, the more the Italian's face seemed to tense up.

"No. Medicina è il TUO sogno! E non coinvolgere la mamma in questa faccenda! La mamma è molto felice che io sia qui. Anche se non è nella stessa città in cui vive lei!" [No. Medicine is YOUR dream! And don't bring Mom into this! Mom is very happy that I'm here. Even if it's not in the same town as her!]

Their eyes had met, but today, no little smile, no eyes going down her body. This stranger seemed clearly pissed off, not in the mood, and despite the fact that she was on the verge of implosion, she was trying not to take it out on the whole bus.

"Sono rimasta a lungo in Italia. Per te! Ma ho una mia vita. Posso fare le mie scelte. E il sangue mi disgusta, ti immagini una chirurgo che sviene ad ogni intervento?!" [I stayed a long time in Italy. For you! But I have my own life. I can make my own choices. And the blood disgusts me, can you imagine a surgeon fainting at every operation?!]

Even though she seemed to be getting annoyed, Maya couldn't help thinking she had the most beautiful voice in the world. She couldn't help thinking her lips were the most beautiful in the world. The way they moved, the way they came forward to form certain words. The way she frowned whenever her interlocutor spoke. And to move her lips forward in a frustrated pout, slightly different from the one when she was concentrating, but just as perfect.

"Ma anche medico di famiglia. Io, non, voglio, essere, nel, settore, medico! È più chiaro così?!" [But even a family doctor. I, don't, want, to, be, in, the, medical, setting! it's clearer this way?!]

Maya couldn't help smiling. Despite the fact that she couldn't understand the conversation, she could see the woman moving her hands as if the person on the other end of the phone could see her.

"Beh, sì! Immagina un po', mi piace vivere a Seattle!" [Well, yes! Believe it or not, I love living in Seattle. !]

Maya, in total indiscretion, had let her gaze fall on this stranger. She must have been in this conversation for a while, because she had just noticed that, compared to other times, she seemed to have a badge around her neck. She couldn't see which company it was, but she probably worked in downtown Seattle.

The Italian finally left the bus, this time not turning around to offer the blonde a smile, or even a glance.

The next day, Maya wondered whether today the Italian would be the charming young lady who had offered her smiles two days earlier, or whether she would be the dark-eyed young woman she'd never want to meet again. Admittedly, she didn't know what it was all about, but even she had been a little scared. The Italian seemed to be a strong-willed woman with her own ideas.

However, after getting on the bus, she would have given anything to run into this woman again, the one from the day before. In fact, the Italian wasn't there today. And in a way, it was as if she'd felt it when the bus stopped in front of her. Her whole body had felt her absence.

She lowered her shoulders before taking out her Marshall headphones, then slipping them over her ears to put on some music.

The next day, once again with her headphones on, Maya boarded the bus. Her heart had gone into extrasystole. She knew she was there, she didn't even need to glance at the bus. She had validated her ticket and as she moved towards the back of the bus, she had reached for the top of her headsets, but she had quickly stopped in her tracks. Indeed, there she was, and Maya's eyes opened wide, her breath catching.

The Italian was dressed in a black trouser suit, white shirt and a thin black tie. A huge smile played across her lips and she ran her hand through her hair to bring back an enormous lock on one side of her face.

Maya's heart had gone into extrasystole. She was sublime. No, she was perfect. But this extrasystole had been painful at the same time. Indeed, that smile wasn't meant for her. Carina was looking at a young man. They were both standing, and the Italian's hand had let go of her lock of hair to slide up the man's neck, then brought him close to her to embrace him. Maya witnessed the whole scene.

"Non puoi immaginare quanto sono felice di vederti! Sono così felice che tu sia venuto a trovarmi." [You can't imagine how happy I am to see you! I'm so happy you've come to see me].

The young man's arms had come to embrace her.

- "Mamma mi ha detto che la telefonata di papà ti ha davvero colpito e, dato che non avevo lezioni fino alla fine della settimana, ho pensato che fosse finalmente giunto il momento di andare a vedere dove viveva la mia sorella maggiore." [Mom told me you'd really been affected after Dad's call, and since I didn't have class until the end of the week, I thought it was finally time to come and see where my big sister lived].

They'd let loose.

- "Sì, ammettilo, sei venuto anche per vedere se il mio appartamento è abbastanza grande perché vuoi venire a stare da me l'anno prossimo se verrai ammesso al Grey Sloan Memorial!" [Yeah, admit it, you're also here to see if my apartment is big enough because you want to crash at my place next year if you get accepted into the residency program at Grey Sloan Memorial!]

- "Puoi dare il benvenuto al tuo fratellino! Anche se dubito che alla mia ragazza piaccia..." [You can welcome your little brother! Even if I doubt my girlfriend will like it...]

- "Per te farei qualsiasi cosa! Non viviamo insieme da troppo tempo! Potrei anche prendere un appartamento più grande, così avremmo ciascuno la propria camera, invece di farti dormire sul mio divano! E poi Gabri potrebbe venire in vacanza o nei fine settimana!" [For you, I'd do anything! We haven't lived together for too long! I could even take a bigger apartment so we could each have our own room, rather than you squatting on my sofa! And then Gabri, she could come on vacation, or for the weekend!]

- "Tipo, vorrai che io stia a casa tua 24 ore su 24, se trovi una bella ragazza americana?!" [Like, you're going to want me at your place 24/7 if you find yourself a pretty little American girl?!]

Carina had blushed, and she'd just remembered that someone hadn't tried to be in her line of vision today. She had turned her head to scan the front of the bus and her eyes had immediately seen her. Maya had tried to turn her head back, but she hadn't been quick enough to avoid being surprised.

The Italian had lowered her face slightly, her cheeks flushing slightly, and her brother Andrea - who preferred to be called Andrew, as he'd been in the States for over ten years for his part - had looked up at the woman who was watching them again.

Carina had quickly grabbed the side of her brother's leather jacket and tugged it to make him turn around.

- "Fermati!"

- "Wow! Aspetta... è davveeeero carina, ma sembra molto giovane, no?! Anche per me!" [Wooow! Wait... she's suuuper pretty, but she looks hyper young, no?! even for me!]

He had turned slightly to detail Maya, who was no longer looking at them, obviously preferring the vision of the bus floor, and Carina had also laid eyes on her, before coming over to grab her brother's chin so he could look at her. Obviously, the blonde hadn't seen everything. Just that moment when the Italian had held the young man's face. They looked at each other, the brunette's cheeks slightly flushed, as if embarrassed and troubled, an identical smile on her lips, still holding the bottom of this man's face.

- "Smettila! Non succederà niente! Ho visto bene che era più giovane. Non so nemmeno se ha diciotto anni." [Stop! Nothing will happen! I saw she was younger. I don't even know if she's eighteen].

She had dropped her face.

"È solo che..." [It's just that...]

- "È solo che ti piacerebbe insegnargli l'educazione sessuale..." [It's just that you'd like to teach her sex education...] Andrew teased her.

Carina had turned red.

- "Fermatiiiiiii" [Stoooooop iittt!] she laughed.

"È semplicemente bellissima. E ogni giorno mi guarda. Davvero, senza alcuna ritrosia. E lo trovo adorabile. Ma sì, è troppo giovane, ne sono consapevole..." [She's just hot. And every day she checking me out. Like really, without any restraint. And I find that adorable. But yes she's too young, I'm aware...]

Her gaze had shifted slightly to Maya and the blonde had managed to look away in time. Carina then bit her lip.

"Ma è davvero un peccato..." [But it's such a shame...]

"Oh... capisco..." [oooh... I see...] Andrew added with a smile.

"È troppo sexy per farlo solo una volta..." [She's too hot to do it to you just once...]

Maya had clenched her jaws and turned up the volume on her headphones.

Maybe the bus wasn't such a good idea after all.

Andrew for his part had grabbed his sister's tie, tugging lightly on it.

- "È andata bene la tua importante presentazione oggi?" [How did your big presentation go today?]

- "Non farlo" [Don't do that.]

- "Hai paura che lei pensi che stiamo flirtando e che in futuro non ti rivolgerà più la parola?" [Are you afraid she'll think we're flirting and won't give you a glance in the future?...] he asked teasingly.

"Ammettilo, sei un po' disgustata dal fatto che sia venuta a prenderti al lavoro perché avresti voluto che ti guardasse vestita così..." [Admit it, you're a little disgusted that I came to pick you up at your job, because you'd have liked to see her checking you out dressed like this...]

The brunette had smiled embarrassedly.

- "La mia presentazione è andata benissimo. Credo che siano rimasti molto soddisfatti del mio lavoro. Era un giorno molto importante e dovevo vestirmi bene. Non so se fosse proprio quello che si aspettava il mio manager quando mi ha detto " domani tailleur "... ma non sono proprio il tipo da tailleur e gonna da hostess. Comunque sia, ieri abbiamo finito un po' più tardi per prepararmi al meglio. E penso di aver fatto davvero un ottimo lavoro!" [My presentation went really well. I think they were very happy with my work. It was a very important day and I had to look good. I don't really know if this is what my manager was expecting when he said «suit up tomorrow»... but I'm not much of a stewardess skirt suit type. Anyway, we finished a little later yesterday to really prepare myself. And I think I did really well].

He had said this with a little smirk, before resuming more seriously.

"Sono orgoglioso di te. E anche la mamma lo è. E sono sicuro che se papà vedesse davvero quanto questo ti rende felice, lo sarebbe anche lui." [I'm proud of you. And so is Mom. And I'm sure if Dad really saw how happy it makes you, he would be too].

E amo quello che faccio" [Proud or not. I don't give a damn. At least he'll have his prodigal son who wants to become a doctor, I don't care if I'm a disappointment to him. You and Mom are enough for me. And I love what I do].

Maya had turned her eyes towards them again. Why hadn't the Italian gotten off at her bus stop? Just as she was about to get off. Two stops after the Italian, she'd seen him wave to the young man she was with.

"Scendiamo qui." [We're getting off here.]

Maya had gotten off through the center door of the bus, while Andrew and Carina had exited through the back door. The blonde had gone to the left, toward Seattle's Auriol Fencing Club. And Carina and her brother, to the right, in the direction of the small local shopping center. Indeed, with her brother coming, she'd wanted to take advantage of his presence to do a little shopping, so extra arms wouldn't go amiss for big purchases.

The next day, Maya had almost deliberately hung around to miss the bus she usually took to the next one. Thus avoiding the beautiful stranger.

Unluckily for her, the Italian must have had the same intention, or else she'd gone out a little late, because when Maya got on the bus, she'd felt this energy squeezing her insides. She hadn't had to look up - but she had anyway - and she'd met her stranger's gaze. Either they'd had the same idea. Or fate was sending her a message.

The brunette had started to smile when she saw Maya looking at her. But the blonde's gaze had been impassive. Her smile didn't take shape and her gaze had turned away. She had gone and sat down - for the first time - with her back to Carina on the bus. Avoiding the temptation to look at her.

The Italian woman had gasped in astonishment, immediately raising and lowering her eyebrows. She lowered her gaze, concentrating on her reading again.

When Carina had returned home. She hadn't even bothered to take off her shoes. She'd crossed the skylight, her coat still on her back, her bag still on her shoulder and she'd dropped onto the sofa, her head hitting the backrest, closing her eyes, all in a desperate and somewhat dramatic gesture.

Andrea, or Andrew, was at the stove, a tea towel over his shoulder. He turned to her. Stopping the wooden spoon he was turning in the pan, he had looked at his sister.

- "No."

She'd made a half-dreamy, half-sulky pout.

"E tu sei un ragazzo!" [And you're a guy!]

And she's not supposed to know you're a lesbian. She might have figured you were bi... or bicurious...]

The brunette had looked at him blasély.

"Tu sei sexy. Io sono sexy. Forse ha pensato che stiamo insieme perché siamo sexy. Basta. Punto! "Period!" [You're hot. I'm hot. Maybe she thought we were together because we're hot. Basta. Punto! «Period!»]

Portare il portafoglio nella tasca posteriore dei pantaloni, attaccato con una catena al passante della cintura? Tenere le chiavi attaccate a un moschettone?" [I've got to, what, wear a Fletcher t-shirt? a rainbow bracelet on my wrist? wear rings on my thumbs? give myself an undercut? carry my wallet in the back pocket of my pants - attached to a chain on my belt loop? have my keys attached to a carabiner].

"Ma, fai un piccolo sforzo..." [But, make a little effort...] he had paused before adding:
